# rearm-barrier

A re-arming broadcast barrier for low-latency benchmarking of tasks across a
fixed set of worker threads. `no_std`, allocation-free, stable Rust.

One producer builds a job, it is broadcast to `WORKERS` consumers behind a
shared reference, every consumer processes it and writes into its own
cache-line-aligned result slot, the producer observes all results, and the
barrier re-arms for the next job. All waiting is by spinning.

Completion is tracked through a tree of counters with fan-in `CLUSTER`, so
finishing workers touch different cache lines instead of one hot counter.

```rust
use rearm_barrier::RearmBarrier;

const WORKERS: usize = 4;
let barrier = RearmBarrier::<u64, u64, WORKERS, 2>::new();

std::thread::scope(|s| {
    for id in 0..WORKERS {
        let barrier = &barrier;
        s.spawn(move || {
            barrier.consumer(100, id, 0, |_version, job, result| {
                *result = job * id as u64;
            });
        });
    }

    barrier.producer(100, |version| version as u64 + 1, |version, results| {
        for (id, r) in results.iter().enumerate() {
            assert_eq!(**r, (version as u64 + 1) * id as u64);
        }
    });
});
```

`RearmBarrier::new` is a `const fn`, so a barrier can live in a `static`.
Invalid configurations (`WORKERS == 0`, `CLUSTER < 2`) are rejected at
compile time.

{"command": "sed -i '' 's/theorem parent_lt (i c : Nat) (hi : 1 ≤ i) (hc : 1 ≤ c) : parent i c < i := by/theorem parent_lt (i c : Nat) (hi : 1 ≤ i) : parent i c < i := by/' RearmBarrier/Tree.lean\ncat > RearmBarrier/Model.lean <<'EOF'\nimport RearmBarrier.Tree\n\n/-!\n# The barrier as a transition system\n\nEvery thread of `RearmBarrier::producer` / `RearmBarrier::consumer` is a\nsmall state machine; the shared memory is the `ticket_probe` counter, the\n`tickets` array, the job slot and the per-worker result slots. A `step`\nexecutes one action of one thread. Actions are\n\n* an atomic read-modify-write (`fetch_add`) or the *successful* load of a\n  spin loop — one action each, matching the atomics in `src/lib.rs`, or\n* half of a non-atomic access: the job write, the result initialisation, the\n  call of `func` and the call of `complete` each take two steps (begin / end)\n  so that overlapping accesses are visible as states.\n\nLoads inside a spin loop that do not satisfy the loop condition are not\nmodelled as steps: the thread is simply not enabled.\n\n## Memory model\n\nThe model interleaves the actions sequentially consistently. This is\njustified for what the model observes: every shared counter is modified only\nby `fetch_add`, so each location has a single modification order that every\nthread agrees on, and the counters only grow, so a stale relaxed load can only\ndelay a thread, never let it through early. The Release/Acquire pairs of the\ncrate order the non-atomic

… [truncated 33115 chars]

### 🤖 **Assistant**  ·  02:30:16; model 13.2s

Done. The Lean model lives in `model/`, the crate is instrumented behind an opt-in `trace` feature, and `scripts/difftest.sh` diff-tests the two. Nothing is committed; everything is in the working tree.

**What the model is**

- `model/RearmBarrier/Tree.lean` mirrors `ticket_tree_alloc` and `ticket_storage` and proves the crate's compile-time sizing claim for all `WORKERS >= 1`, `CLUSTER >= 2`, that the ticket walk terminates, and that its starting index is in bounds. These are real proofs, no `sorry`, no Mathlib.
- `Model.lean` is the transition system: one step per `fetch_add` or successful spin-loop exit, with the non-atomic job and result accesses split into begin/end steps so overlaps show up as states. The header documents why sequentially consistent interleaving is sound for the counters the model observes.
- `Spec.lean` states the promises: `func` sees its version's job, `complete` sees every result, no conflicting slot accesses overlap, the walk stays in bounds, counters never run ahead.
- `Explore.lean` exhaustively enumerates interleavings and checks invariants plus deadlock freedom. `Trace.lean` replays a crate trace and decides whether it is an execution of the model, using a greedy scheduler whose completeness argument is in the file.

**How the diff test works**

The crate reports every atomic operation to a hook when the `trace` feature is on. Without the feature the hook is an empty inline function. `examples/trace.rs` records per-thread event sequences on real threads with optional random delays, and the Lean checker must reproduce exactly the same operations from the same observed counter values. If the crate hangs, the harness dumps a partial trace and the checker says whether the model agrees it is a deadlock.

**Results**

| Check | Outcome |
|---|---|
| `ticket_storage`, crate vs model | 61,440 shapes agree |
| Exhaustive exploration, 10 small shapes | no violation, up to 1.2M states for 7 workers |
| Crate traces replayed on the model | 140 per run, 0 failures, also 0 failures in a 40-seed run up to 17 workers |
| `cargo test`, feature off and on | 14 pass |

**Mutation checks.** I injected bugs to confirm the test has teeth. In the crate: consumers starting one probe value early, and forwarding the wrong merge amount, were both pinpointed to the exact divergent operation. In the model: a producer that does not wait, a consumer that forgets to bump the probe, and a root that finishes early were caught by exploration as a race, a deadlock, and a wrong job respectively.

**Limits.** The model cannot see memory-ordering bugs such as a missing fence, and it does not model the duplicate-producer panic paths. A longer fuzz run with 30 seeds per shape is running in the background and will report when it finishes.

### 👤 **User**  ·  02:31:27; waited 1m11s

how to fit memory ordering into this?

### 🤖 **Assistant**  ·  02:32:35; model 1m24s

> 💭 (thinking)
> 

Short answer: keep the interleaving model, and layer a happens-before tracker on top of it using vector clocks. That is the established pattern for C11-style orderings. Miri's data-race detector and loom both do exactly this, and it drops into the current design without touching the harness.

**Why this is the right fit.** The interleaving over the counters is already exact, because every write is a read-modify-write and those have a single modification order per location. What the model lacks is the synchronizes-with relation those operations create for the non-atomic job and result slots. Right now `Spec.lean` calls something a race when two conflicting accesses overlap in an interleaving. The real definition is "two conflicting accesses not ordered by happens-before", which is stronger: it flags a missing fence even in an interleaving where the accesses happen not to overlap. C11's DRF theorem then says that if every sequentially consistent execution is race-free under that definition, the program has only SC executions, so the model's SC interleaving is justified rather than assumed.

**What changes in the model.**

- Each thread carries a vector clock. Each atomic location (the probe and every ticket) carries a release clock. Each non-atomic slot (the job, each result) carries the clock of its last write and a per-thread read clock, FastTrack style.
- Every step gets the ordering the crate actually uses. A Release `fetch_add` merges the thread's clock into the location's release clock. An AcqRel `fetch_add` on a ticket first joins the location's release clock into the thread, then releases. The relaxed spin load followed by `fence(Acquire)` joins the release clock of the write it read into the thread. Since all writes are RMWs, release sequences are just "the location's release clock accumulates", which is the rule Miri implements.
- Non-atomic accesses become checks: a write must see the slot's last write and all reads in its clock, a read must see the last write. Failure is a data race with a counterexample path from the explorer.
- The existing overlap check stays as a sanity invariant but is subsumed.

Cost is roughly two hundred lines of Lean and a larger state space, because clocks distinguish states that were previously merged. The shapes the explorer runs today should still fit; the ten-worker shapes were already over budget anyway.

**What changes on the crate side.** Nothing in the trace harness, because hardware on this machine will not exhibit a missing fence and the trace format has no ordering information. The runtime oracle for orderings is Miri: `cargo miri test` runs the crate under the same vector-clock semantics, including fences and release sequences. That gives a genuine differential test for memory ordering: delete the acquire fence in `consumer`, and both the Lean explorer and Miri must report a race on the job slot at the same access. Loom is the alternative, but it requires swapping in its atomic types behind a feature and adding yields inside the spin loops, so Miri is the less invasive second oracle.

**What this still would not cover.** Stale relaxed reads that change control flow. For this barrier they cannot, since every spin condition is monotone in a counter that only grows, so a stale value only delays a thread. That argument is currently prose in `Model.lean`; with clocks in place it could become a stated lemma. A full axiomatic C11 or promising-semantics formalization is not practical here, since nothing like that exists as a Lean 4 library.

If you want, I can implement the vector-clock layer, add the Miri run to `scripts/difftest.sh`, and repeat the fence-removal mutation on both sides to show they agree.

### 🤖 **Assistant**  ·  02:43:29; model 11.0s

All three items are done. The memory-ordering work is in the working tree, uncommitted, since you asked for the commit before it.

**1. Commit.** Committed on `main` as `b32bde4`, since the repo has no remote. It contains only sources: the Lean files, `lakefile.toml`, `lean-toolchain` and `lake-manifest.json`. `model/.lake/` is git-ignored, and `git check-ignore` confirms the `rearm-model.hash` file you saw is covered. That "Updated" line is Lake's build log, not git.

**2. Miri.** All 13 tests and the doctest pass under `cargo miri test`, with its data race detector and weak-memory emulation on. The lib tests took about 71 seconds.

**3. Memory ordering in the model.** `Model.lean` now tracks C11 happens-before with vector clocks on top of the interleaving:

- Every thread has a clock, and the probe and every ticket have a release clock. A `fetch_add` joins clocks according to its ordering, and the release clock accumulates over the modification order, which gives release-sequence semantics for free since every write is a read-modify-write.
- A spin-loop exit acquires the release clock of the write it read only if the corresponding `fence(Acquire)` is present.
- The job slot and each result slot keep a last-write epoch and per-thread read clocks. A non-atomic access that is not ordered after every conflicting earlier one is a data race step, which the explorer reports with a counterexample path.
- The crate's orderings are the defaults of a new `Orderings` structure, and `rearm-model explore` accepts `publish`, `ticket`, `finish`, `producer-fence` or `consumer-fence` to weaken one.

**Results.** Every shape in the diff-test script still passes, and the 140 replayed crate traces are still accepted. Weakening any one of the five orderings makes exploration fail with a race naming the two accesses. Removing the consumer's Acquire fence in a scratch copy of the crate makes Miri report the same race, between the job write in `producer` and the `&*self.val_ptr()` read in `consumer`, so the two oracles agree.

**One cost.** Clock tracking distinguishes which consumers have finished rather than how many, so the state space grew about four times. The six-worker fan-in-eight shape in the script now runs one version instead of two, as it would exceed the two-million-state budget otherwise.

### 🤖 **Assistant**  ·  02:46:26; model 51.2s

> 💭 (thinking)
> 

Honest assessment: a full parametric proof (every `WORKERS`, `CLUSTER`, `count`, every interleaving) is a substantial project, on the order of weeks for someone fluent in Lean, not days. Nothing about it is a research problem, but the volume is real. Here is where the work sits.

**What the theorem is.** An inductive invariant `Inv cfg s` such that the initial state satisfies it, every `step` preserves it, and no state satisfying it can take a `.race` step (or `.fault`, or deadlock). Finding and proving that invariant is the whole job. It has two halves.

- **The counting half.** Which consumers a ticket node covers, that its value equals the sum of contributions from its subtree, that a window "fills" exactly when every consumer in it has finished the version, and hence that `new_val == WORKERS * (version + 1)` happens exactly once per version, after every consumer's `func`. This requires formalizing the heap layout, window sizes and levels of the tree, which the model currently only computes. This is the part most likely to hide a real bug, and it is also provable in isolation as pure arithmetic, with no concurrency involved.
- **The happens-before half.** That when a subtree's window fills, the ticket's release clock dominates every consumer's clock at its result write, that this chains up to the probe, and that the fences carry it into the producer and back down to the consumers for the next version. Given the counting invariant, this is a mechanical induction over the clock-join rules, but every step of the model touches arrays with `set!`, `modify` and `zipWith`, and those lemmas are tedious.

**The practical obstacle is the model's style, not the mathematics.** It is written as an executable function over arrays for fast replay. Proving things about it directly means fighting array indexing in every case. The standard route is to write a relational or `Fin`-indexed version, prove the executable one refines it, and do the real proof on the clean one. That refactor alone is a few days.

**What stays outside Lean either way.** The C11 DRF-SC theorem, that hb-race-freedom in all SC executions rules out weak behaviors, would remain a cited result. Formalizing C11 is out of scope for any sensible plan.

**Cheaper options with real value.**

1. Bounded theorems by `native_decide`: `explore {workers := 5, cluster := 2, count := 2} = ok` as a Lean theorem. Machine-checked for that shape, an hour of work, trusting the compiler and the explorer code. This turns the current script output into checked statements.
2. Unbounded `count` for a fixed shape via a periodicity lemma: reachable states at version `v + 1` are shifts of those at version `v`. This would be tractable without clocks. The clock layer breaks uniform shifting, because a thread's tick count per version depends on how far its walk went, so this would need a quotient on clocks first.
3. Prove only the counting lemma parametrically. A few hundred lines, and it is the piece whose proof would actually tell you something new.

My recommendation, if you want to invest: do option 1 now, then the counting lemma, and only then decide whether the full invariant is worth the refactor.

### 🤖 **Assistant**  ·  02:52:27; model 5m48s

This is a good direction: a structural tree makes the update a well-defined operation on an inductive type, and it gives a second, independent implementation of the completion logic that the existing one can be checked against. Here is the plan before I write code:

- Make the model generic over a **ticket engine**: the producer, the hb tracking, the explorer, the replayer and the CLI stay shared. The flat heap array becomes one engine, the tree another, and a **product engine** runs both in lockstep and faults if they ever disagree on a ticket id, amount, old value, clock or decision. Exploring the product is a bisimulation check over every interleaving; replaying a crate trace on it checks both models.
- The tree is `Tree.mk count rel lo hi children`: each node owns a consumer window, its window size *is* the target value, and a consumer's cursor is a path from the root. The update is `modifyAt path`, which cannot change the shape.
- Prove the layout correspondence, `heapIndex (p ++ [k]) = C * heapIndex p + k + 1` and its parent lemma, so the tree's event ids are the flat heap's ids by construction.

### 🤖 **Assistant**  ·  03:02:32; model 12.5s

The tree model is in place, using your node state, and it agrees with the flat model everywhere I could check. Changes are uncommitted.

**What changed**

- The model is now generic over a **ticket engine**. The producer, happens-before tracking, explorer, replayer and CLI are shared. `Flat.lean` is the crate's array and arithmetic, `TreeModel.lean` is the structural tree, and `Product.lean` runs both in lockstep and faults on any disagreement in ticket id, amount, old value, clock or the consumer's next move. `rearm-model [flat|tree|both] ...` selects the engine, default `both`.
- **Tree nodes** carry `(version, finished)` over a consumer window `[lo, hi)`. In-flight is `size - finished`, the crate's counter is `size * version + finished`, a fill moves the node to `(version + 1, 0)`, and the consumer that filled it carries `size` to the parent. Your "root is finished when finished = workers" became "the node covering every worker fills", because the crate short-circuits there and never touches the ancestors. The update is `modifyAt path`, so the shape cannot change.
- **The counting invariant is now stated and machine-checked** in every explored state: a leaf's `finished` equals the number of its consumers past their leaf `fetch_add`; a live inner node's `finished` plus contributions in flight towards it equals the total size of children one version ahead; no child or consumer is more than one version ahead; dead ancestors stay untouched; a consumer only contributes to a node counting its own version. This is the inductive invariant a parametric proof would need, so it is now a concrete, tested statement rather than a sketch.
- Two lemmas tie the tree to the heap layout: `heapIndex (p ++ [k]) = C * heapIndex p + k + 1` and that `(i - 1) / C` undoes it.

**Evidence**

| Check | Result |
|---|---|
| Product engine, exhaustive, 12 shapes up to 1.15M states | identical state counts to flat alone, no disagreement |
| Random schedules, 14 shapes up to 17 workers, including layouts with dead heap nodes | 112 runs, no disagreement, invariant intact |
| Crate traces replayed through both engines | 140, all accepted |
| Weakened orderings on the tree engine alone | still caught |
| Invariant deliberately broken in a scratch copy | fails on the first state, so the check is live |

I kept `version` in the node instead of resetting to `(size, 0)`, because after a reset the parent can no longer tell which children have filled for its current version, and the cross-level invariant needs that. The trade-off is one extra number per node.

### 🤖 **Assistant**  ·  03:14:21; model 13.7s

Both parts are done. The tree engine is committed as `1b7da62`, and the new formal development is in the working tree, uncommitted.

**What is now proved, for every tree shape and every ordering**

`model/RearmBarrier/Completion.lean` isolates one version's completion as a game on the tree. Consumers are unit leaves. The two moves are a consumer finishing, and a pending child being applied to its parent, which adds the child's size and joins the child's clock into the parent's, exactly what the `AcqRel` `fetch_add` does. Either move can happen at any node at any time, so every interleaving of the crate's ticket walks is a run of the game. The theorems, all closed with no `sorry` and depending only on `propext`, `Quot.sound` and `Classical.choice`:

- `Step.inv` and `Fresh.inv`: the counting and clock invariant holds initially and is preserved by every move.
- `done_contributed`: a done node has every consumer below it finished. So the root fills only after every consumer's `fetch_add`.
- `done_clock`: a done node's clock dominates every consumer's clock below it. This is the happens-before edge from every result write to the completing `fetch_add`, which is the memory-ordering claim you asked about.
- `noStep_of_done`: nothing happens at or below a done node again. The barrier completes exactly once per version.
- `progress` and `Step.measure_lt`: once every consumer has finished, a move is possible while the root is not done, and every move decreases a measure. Hence `terminal_done` and `run_completes`: any ordering of completion events finishes, with all clocks acquired.

**What remains checked rather than proved**, and why:

- That the executable tree engine is an instance of the game, and that the flat engine matches it. The game abstracts consumer identity and uses a relational step, while the engines are executable array-and-path code. The link is validated by the product engine over every interleaving of small shapes and by 140 crate traces. Proving it means a refinement proof against `modifyAt`, which is mechanical but long.
- The hop from the completing `fetch_add` to `complete`: one Release on the probe and one Acquire fence, plus the symmetric hop from the job write to `func`. These are two clock joins over a single location, easy to state, but they live in the multi-version model, which brings back the version arithmetic. The game is single-version and never re-arms.
- The per-version re-arm. Extending the game to versions is the natural next step, and the `(version, finished)` node state was chosen so that the invariant stays uniform there.

One thing to note: the proof of `done_clock` depends on the `AcqRel` ordering. With a relaxed ticket `fetch_add` the join in `Step.apply` would not exist and the theorem would be false, which matches what exploration reported when that ordering was weakened.

### 👤 **User**  ·  03:16:29; waited 2m08s

### 🤖 **Assistant**  ·  03:16:36; model 7.1s

"Game" was loose wording. I mean a small nondeterministic transition system, not anything game-theoretic.

Concretely, `Completion.lean` defines a relation `Step : Node → Node → Prop` with three rules:

- `finish`: a leaf (one consumer) goes from counter 0 to counter 1 and becomes pending. This is the consumer's own `fetch_add` on its leaf ticket.
- `apply`: a child that is pending gets added to its parent. The parent's counter grows by the child's size, its clock absorbs the child's clock, the child stops being pending, and the parent becomes pending if it just filled. This is the walking consumer's `fetch_add` one level up.
- `inner`: either of the above happening somewhere inside a child.

Nothing chooses which rule fires or where. Any node where a rule's precondition holds may step next, so a "run" is just any sequence of steps, and that is what stands in for "any ordering of completion events". The theorems quantify over all such sequences.

I called it a game because a run is like taking turns with an adversarial scheduler, but "abstract completion protocol" or "one-version transition system" is the accurate name. I'll rename it in the file header and README if you prefer.

### 🤖 **Assistant**  ·  03:27:00; model 11.3s

All four items are done. The "game" clarification and the completion proofs are committed as `36a807c`. Items 2 to 4 are in the working tree, uncommitted, since you asked for a commit only on the first.

**2. The diff fuzzer now runs on the tree.** There is a single model again, with the tree as its ticket state. Crate traces still name tickets by heap index, so the two-way mapping does the translation:

- `heapIndex` and `pathOfIndex` convert between a node's path and its heap index. Both directions are proved inverse, `heapIndex_pathOfIndex` for every index and `pathOfIndex_heapIndex` for every path with digits below the fan-in, with only `propext` and `Quot.sound`.
- `Tree.toCounters` renders the tree as the crate's counter array and `Tree.ofCounters` reads one back, using `(version, finished) = (counter / size, counter % size)`. A replayed crate trace ends with the same final array the flat model used to print, `#[15, 12, 3, 6, 6, 3]` for the 5-worker example, and the round trip through `ofCounters` reproduces the tree.

**3. The flat version is deleted**, along with the product engine. The crate's own arithmetic remains only in `Tree.lean`, for the storage-size proofs.

**4. The engine abstraction is gone.** `State` holds a `Tree` directly, `ConsumerPhase` carries a path cursor, and the explorer, replayer and CLI are no longer generic. Shared definitions moved to a new `Basics.lean`.

Validation after the restructure, all with the tree model alone:

| Check | Result |
|---|---|
| Exhaustive exploration, 11 shapes, up to 181k states | no violation, same state counts as before |
| Each of the 5 weakened orderings | reported as a data race |
| Random schedules, 13 shapes up to 17 workers | 78 runs, no failure |
| Crate traces via `scripts/difftest.sh` | 140 accepted, storage table agrees on 61,440 shapes |

One thing lost with the flat engine: the bisimulation check between the two representations. What now ties the tree to the crate's layout is the proved index mapping plus the crate traces, which fail the replay if any ticket id, amount or old value differs from what the crate did.

### 🤖 **Assistant**  ·  03:30:06; model 31.1s

> 💭 (thinking)
> 

You're right, and I overstated it. Let me separate what the product engine actually checked from what remains.

The product engine compared, at every step of every interleaving of a small shape, two Lean implementations: the tree and my transcription of the crate's `win_id` / `win_size` / `(i - 1) / C` arithmetic. It was a bisimulation between the tree and a copy of the crate's logic, not between the tree and the crate.

What the correspondence consists of, and how each piece is covered now:

- **Which node is which ticket.** Proved, for every path and index, by the two mapping lemmas. The product engine only checked this for explored shapes.
- **Which value the crate's counter holds.** `counter = size * version + finished`. Every `ticket` event in a crate trace carries the crate's real `old`, and the replay fails unless the tree computes the same value. So this is checked against the actual crate, not a transcription.
- **What the consumer does next.** The amount it adds next and the ticket it touches next are the next `ticket` event of that thread, or its `finish` event, so a wrong decision fails the replay one event later. Less direct than the product's step-level comparison, but the same information.
- **Independence from the interleaving.** The one thing the exhaustive product check had over traces was coverage of every ordering. But the correspondence above is order-independent as long as the counting invariant holds, and that invariant is what the explorer checks exhaustively in every state, and what `Completion.lean` proves for a single version.

So the content of the check is preserved, and in one respect improved, since the reference is now the crate rather than my reading of it. The residual differences are minor: trace coverage of orderings comes from the scheduler and jitter rather than enumeration, and a decision mismatch is reported at the following event rather than at the step itself. I should have said that instead of "lost".

### 🤖 **Assistant**  ·  03:31:44; model 14.9s

Here is the honest inventory. Everything below is `sorry`-free and uses only `propext`, `Quot.sound` and, in one theorem, `Classical.choice`.

**About the executable tree in `TreeModel.lean`: only the layout.**

- `heapIndex_append`: the index of a child path is `C * index(parent) + k + 1`, the crate's heap rule.
- `parent_heapIndex`: the crate's `(i - 1) / C` undoes that.
- `heapIndex_pathOfIndex` and `pathOfIndex_heapIndex`: the path-to-index and index-to-path maps are mutually inverse, for every index and for every path with digits below the fan-in.

Nothing about `Tree.walk`, `Tree.build`, `modifyAt` or `Tree.invariant` is proved. In particular, there is no theorem that `build` produces windows matching the crate's `win_id * win_size` arithmetic, or that `walk` preserves `Tree.invariant`. Those are only checked by exploration and by trace replay.

**About the crate's sizing arithmetic in `Tree.lean`.**

- `ticketStorage_le`: `ticket_storage(w, c) ≤ 2 * w` for every `w ≥ 1`, `c ≥ 2`, the assertion the crate checks per instantiation.
- `parent_lt` and `start_ticket_lt`: the walk's index strictly decreases and starts inside the array, so every walk terminates in bounds.

**About the abstract one-version protocol in `Completion.lean`.** This is a separate inductive `Node` type with a relational `Step`, not the executable `Tree`. For every shape and every run:

- `Fresh.inv`, `Step.inv`, `Steps.inv`: the counting and clock invariant holds initially and is preserved.
- `done_contributed`: a done node has every consumer under it finished.
- `done_clock`: a done node's clock dominates every consumer's clock under it. This is the happens-before result, and it depends on the join in `Step.apply`, that is, on the `AcqRel` ordering.
- `noStep_of_done`: nothing happens at or under a done node again.
- `progress`, `Step.measure_lt`, `terminal_done`, `run_completes`: once every consumer has finished, a step is possible until the root is done, every step decreases a measure, so every maximal run ends done with all clocks acquired.

**The gap.** There is no proof connecting `Completion.Node` to `TreeModel.Tree`: no refinement showing that `Tree.walk` on a tree from `Tree.build` is a `Completion.Step`, and no multi-version statement at all. The executable model's own invariant is the same statement as `Completion.Inv` in spirit, but that is by inspection, not by theorem. So the precise claim is: the protocol the tree implements is proved correct for one version; that the tree implements it is tested.

If you want to close that gap, the natural next theorem is `walk_refines`: for a well-formed tree at a uniform version, `Tree.walk` at a cursor corresponds to one `Completion.Step` on the projection that forgets versions and clocks. That would also require `build_wf`, which is the missing link to the crate's window arithmetic.

### 🤖 **Assistant**  ·  03:47:11; model 17.7s

Done. The single-implementation restructure is committed as `f4ef892`; the new proofs are in the working tree, uncommitted.

**What is now proved about the executable tree** (`model/RearmBarrier/TreeProofs.lean`, no `sorry`, standard axioms only):

- **`modifyAt` is a well-defined update.** `get_modifyAt_self`: it changes exactly the node at its path. `get_window_modifyAt`: if the function keeps a node's window and children, no window anywhere in the tree changes.
- **`Tree.walk` is one `fetch_add`.** `walk_spec`: on success there is a node at the cursor's path, at the consumer's version; the event carries the crate's heap index, the cursor's amount and the node's counter as `old`; afterwards that node's counter is `old + amount`; no window changes; and the next move is `finished` iff the node filled and covers every worker, `stop` iff it did not fill, `continue` iff it filled without covering every worker, carrying `(parent path, size)`.
- **The decision is the crate's.** `walk_crate`: translating the tree's next move through `heapIndex` gives exactly `if new_val == WORKERS * (v + 1) { finished } else if ticket_id == 0 || new_val != target_val * (v + 1) { stop } else { continue at (ticket_id - 1) / CLUSTER with target_val }`, for any node covering at most every worker. This is the theorem that replaces the old flat engine.
- **`build` and the root.** `build_lo`, `build_hi`: a node of height `h` at `lo` covers `[lo, lo + C^(h+1)) ∩ [0, W)`. `pow_levels_ge` characterises the crate's `ticket_tree_alloc` loop exit, and `init_root_size`: for every valid configuration the root covers exactly `[0, WORKERS)`, so `walk` can never hit its root fault.

`Tree.invariant` itself is a `partial def`, which Lean treats as opaque, so nothing can be proved about it as written. The explorer still checks it in every state.

**What is missing to connect `Completion.Node` to `TreeModel.Tree`**, in order of dependency:

1. **`build` well-formedness.** That the children of a built node partition its window consecutively, leaves have at most `C` consumers, inner nodes at most `C` children. Provable but tedious, because `build` uses `List.range C |>.filterMap`. Without it there is no proof that `target_val` sums correctly across a level.
2. **A restated invariant.** `Tree.invariant` must become a `Prop` on `(Tree, consumer phases)` by structural recursion, so that "`step` preserves it" is a theorem. `walk_spec` already gives the per-node facts that proof needs.
3. **The projection.** A relation between `(Tree, consumer phases)` at version `v` and a `Completion.Node`: a crate leaf becomes a node with unit children, one per consumer, finished iff the consumer is past its leaf `fetch_add`; a child is `pending` iff some consumer carries its size to the parent; clocks map `VC` (arrays) to `Clock` (functions). It has to be a relation, not a function, because the cursor records only `(parent, size)`, not which sibling it came from, and siblings can share a size.
4. **The refinement lemma.** Each `walk` step maps to one or two `Completion.Step`s under that relation: a leaf `fetch_add` is `finish` followed by `apply`, an inner one is `apply`. `walk_spec` plus the well-formedness from item 1 should be enough.
5. **Versions.** The game is single-version. The multi-version model needs: all live nodes are at version `v` with `finished = 0` when version `v` is published, which is `Fresh`, and the probe hop from the completing `fetch_add` to the producer's `complete` (one release, one acquire fence) for the memory-ordering conclusion.

Item 1 is the real work and unblocks the rest; items 3 and 4 are mostly bookkeeping once the invariant is a `Prop`.

### 🤖 **Assistant**  ·  03:53:03; model 12.5s

Both parts are done. The earlier proofs are committed as `06ae03e`; the well-formedness work is in the working tree, uncommitted.

**What changed in the model.** `build` no longer produces its children through `filterMap` over `List.range`. It uses a small recursive `buildChildren` that adds slots left to right and stops at the first one starting at or beyond `WORKERS`. The result is the same tree, since once a slot is missing all later ones are too, and the executable checks confirm it: identical state counts on the exhaustive shapes, no failures on random schedules up to 17 workers, and all 140 crate traces still accepted.

**What is proved** (`TreeProofs.lean`, no `sorry`, standard axioms):

- `Wf C W t`: every node covers a non-empty window inside `[0, W)`, a leaf covers at most `C` consumers, and an inner node has at most `C` children whose windows tile its own window consecutively (`Tiles`).
- `Wf_build`: `build` produces such a tree for every height and every start below `W`. The core lemma is `buildChildren_tiles`: the slots from `k` on tile `[min(lo + k·w, W), min(lo + (k+n)·w, W))`, which absorbs the truncated last child.
- `init_wf`: the tree of every valid configuration is well formed.
- `Wf.sum_children`: an inner node's window is the sum of its children's, via `tiles_sum`. This is the "`target_val` adds up" fact the counting argument needs.
- `Wf.get`, `Wf.digits`, `Wf.size_le`, `Wf.size_pos`: every node reachable by a path is well formed, its path digits are below `C`, and its size is between 1 and `W`.
- `Wf.modifyAt` and `tiles_set`: a window-preserving update keeps the tree well formed. `walk_wf`: so does every walk step, using a new conjunct of `walk_spec` that exhibits the walk's result as such an update.
- `walk_crate_wf`: on a well-formed tree the decision theorem holds with no side conditions, so it applies along every execution from `Tree.init`.

**What this closes and what remains.** Item 1 of the gap list is done: the shape of the executable tree matches the crate's layout by proof, not by testing. The remaining pieces are the ones I listed before it: restating `Tree.invariant` as a `Prop` so that "`step` preserves it" can be a theorem, the projection relation from `(Tree, consumer phases)` to `Completion.Node`, the one-or-two-step refinement lemma, and the multi-version re-arm argument. `Wf.sum_children` and `walk_spec` are the ingredients those proofs will consume.

theorem leafDone_nextPhase (cfg : Config) (v : Nat) (next : Next) (q : List Nat) (hv : v < cfg.count)
    (h : ∀ c', next = .continue c' → c'.path ≠ q) :
    leafDone q cfg.count (nextPhase cfg v next) = v + 1 := by
  cases next with
  | finished => rfl
  | stop => exact leafDone_nextConsumer cfg q v hv
  | «continue» c' =>
    have := h c' rfl
    simp [nextPhase, this]

theorem nextPhase_walk {cfg : Config} {V : Nat} {next : Next} {v' : Nat} {c' : Cursor}
    (h : nextPhase cfg V next = .walk v' c') : next = .continue c' ∧ v' = V := by
  cases next with
  | finished => simp [nextPhase] at h
  | stop =>
    unfold nextPhase nextConsumer at h
    split at h <;> simp at h
  | «continue» c'' =>
    simp only [nextPhase, ConsumerPhase.walk.injEq] at h

### 🤖 **Assistant**  ·  04:20:06; model 16.4s

The well-formedness work is committed as `1712b6b`. The invariant-preservation proofs are in the working tree, uncommitted.

**What is proved** (`model/RearmBarrier/TreeInvariant.lean`, 1,544 lines, no `sorry`, standard axioms):

`Tree.invariant` is a `partial def`, so I restated it as a proposition `TreeInv cfg V phase t`. It is indexed by paths, with a node's children addressed as `path ++ [k]`, so no induction over the nested tree is ever needed. Consumers are a function `Nat → ConsumerPhase`, so a step is a point update. The clauses are the ones the executable check enforces: carried contributions are for the node's version, counters never fill, versions sit in `[V, V+1]`, a leaf counts consumers past their leaf `fetch_add`, a live inner node's counter plus what is carried towards it is the size of its children one version ahead, dead nodes are untouched, plus cursor shape facts (a walker's cursor is on the path to its leaf, carried amounts are positive, `Cursor.start` names the unique leaf holding a consumer).

Every consumer step preserves it:

- `walk_preserves`: the `fetch_add` on a ticket. This is the substantive one, split into the node under the cursor (`nodeInv_self`), its parent (`nodeInv_parent`), and every other node (`nodeInv_other`). It contains the counting argument: when a leaf fills, every consumer of its window is past its `fetch_add`; when an inner node fills, everything carried towards it was this consumer's and every child is one version ahead; when the consumer carries to the parent, the parent is live, at the consumer's version, and its filled-children sum grows by exactly the child's size.
- `phase_preserves`: `start → initializing → waitReady`, `waitReady → beforeFunc → inFunc`, `finish → waitReady/done`.
- `startWalk_preserves`: `inFunc V → walk V (Cursor.start id)`, which needs the leaf to be at version `V`; that follows from the invariant alone.
- `advance_preserves`: once every consumer is in `waitReady (V+1)` or `done`, the invariant holds at `V+1`. Its core, `all_advanced`, shows by strong induction on subtree size that every live node has moved to `V+1`.

The only hypotheses are what the global protocol supplies: the acting consumer's version is the producer's current `V`, and `V < count`.

**What is still missing** to state this as "`step` on `State` preserves the invariant":

1. The global protocol invariant tying the probe value, the producer's phase and each consumer's phase to `V`, so that those hypotheses hold at every step and `advance_preserves` fires exactly at `completing V → beforeWrite (V+1)`. That is the remaining "counting half" I estimated earlier, but it is now a bookkeeping proof about phases and the probe, with the tree work done.
2. The initial state: `NodeInv` at `Tree.init` is routine, but `leaves` and `leaf_unique` need two more `build` lemmas, that `Cursor.start id` reaches the leaf whose window holds `id` and that no other leaf does.
3. The bridge from `Array.setIfInBounds` in `State.setConsumer` to the function update `upd`, which is a small lemma.

Item 1 is the real remaining work; items 2 and 3 are short.

### 🤖 **Assistant**  ·  04:33:48; model 11.7s

Both items are done. The invariant-preservation work is committed as `8dec8ff`; items 2 and 3 are in the working tree, uncommitted. All new theorems are `sorry`-free on standard axioms, and the executable model still validates identically (exhaustive shapes, random schedules to 17 workers, weakened orderings caught, 140 crate traces accepted).

**Item 2, the initial state** (`TreeInit.lean`):

- `pathAt` is now defined by recursion on the depth, the base-`C` digits of `j`, with the same values as the old fold. `access` writes the result slots through a named loop, `writeAllSlots`, with the same behaviour, which makes the step lemmas below tractable.
- `get_build_char`: every node of a built tree is determined by its path. The node at `q` is `build (H - |q|)` at `digits q * C^(H - |q| + 1)`, every digit is below `C`, and `|q| ≤ H`.
- `init_leaves`: `Cursor.start id` names a leaf whose window holds `id`. `init_leaf_unique`: no other leaf holds it, because a leaf must sit at depth `H` and its window pins the digits to `id / C`.
- `init_treeInv`: the invariant holds at version 0 with every consumer in `start`.

**Item 3, the bridge to `State`** (`StepInvariant.lean`):

- `phaseOf` views the consumer array as a function, and `phaseOf_setIfInBounds` shows `setConsumer` is the point update `upd` the invariant is stated over.
- `step_consumer_move` decomposes every consumer step of `step` into one of seven moves plus a `setConsumer`, with the walk move carrying the actual `Tree.walk` equation. It goes through the race checks and the clock bookkeeping without touching them.
- `step_consumer_treeInv`: every consumer step of `step` preserves `TreeInv`, given that a consumer inside `func`, walking, or finishing is at the producer's version `V` and `V < count`.
- `step_producer_treeInv`: producer steps leave the tree and consumers alone.

**What is now the single remaining piece** for "`step` preserves the invariant" as one theorem: the global protocol invariant relating the probe, the producer's phase and the consumers' phases to `V`. It has to supply the version hypotheses above and identify the moment the producer advances, where `advance_preserves` takes over. That is bookkeeping over phases and the probe with no tree reasoning left in it.
